MimirAlloc Z13 recorded run - Z-019 lock hold and wait histograms
slice: Z-019 - Lock hold and wait histograms (debug-build-only rdtsc instrumentation of core lock hold/wait per public entry point; zero cost when not compiled in)
date: 2026-11-12T03:17:47-03:18:18-05:00 (histogram run window, America/New_York); gate suite run minutes earlier the same morning
base: 09b8e55 (branch yrsa/mimiralloc-team-05, Z-014 commit; seat main at build time had Z-001..Z-005 banked, Z-006..Z-018 in flight or queued)
host: purpleraider (ASUS ROG Flow Z13, AMD Strix Halo, x86-64, 4 KiB pages, 128 GB UMA)
artifacts: src/core/mimir_core.c + src/core/mimir_core.h (instrumentation, MIMIR_LOCK_HIST only), tests/perf/probe_z019.c, tests/perf/run_lockhist_z019.sh, tests/perf/summarize_z019.py, this recorded run.
method (per docs/Z13_MEASUREMENT_PROTOCOL.md, Z-004 banked at the seat): every measured value is a separate PROCESS; single-thread phases pinned taskset -c 2 (CCD0 physical core); contended phases pinned taskset -c 0-7 (one whole CCD, one thread per physical core, no SMT siblings); 3 warm-up processes per phase discarded; 7 measured processes per phase; headline = MEDIAN across processes of per-process scalars (mean / p50 / p90 / p99 from that process's buckets); pooled buckets (summed across processes) are printed for shape only and are never the headline. Histograms are reset after each phase's in-process warm-up, so only timed work is recorded. Checksum discipline (K-002): exactly one checksum per bench workload across all 7 processes - status OK for all four workloads below. Drift: split-half drift of per-process mean hold/wait is printed per series; every headline series is <= 3.0% against the protocol's 8% bound.
instrumentation: src/core/mimir_core.c Z-019 section, compiled ONLY with -DMIMIR_LOCK_HIST=1 (the runner builds it into build/z19/; the production Makefile never defines the macro). Within that translation unit the two L6 lock calls are shadowed by static wrappers: wait = rdtsc (LFENCE-fenced) just before the real pthread lock call to just after it returns; hold = stamp just after acquisition (taken after the wait sample is recorded, so the wait update is not charged to the hold) to just before the real unlock call. Both samples are recorded while the thread holds the one global lock, so the shared arrays need no atomics. Per-entry attribution is a thread-local tag set at the top of mimir_core_alloc / _calloc / _realloc / _free and cleared to OTHER by the unlock wrapper; realloc(NULL) delegates to the alloc entry before either locks, so the inner entry owns that sample (the E-005 call accounting). Buckets are quarter-octave: index < 64 is the exact cycle count; index >= 64 covers [2^e + sub*2^(e-2), +2^(e-2)) with e = 6 + (idx-64)/4, sub = (idx-64) % 4 (zero width error below 64 cycles, at most 25% above); count / sum / min / max are exact. Snapshot and reset take no lock and are called only at quiescent points, so reading never pollutes the measurement. Failed lock acquisitions record no sample (lock_failures counter: 0 in every process of this run). Samples with negative or >= 2^48-cycle deltas (TSC artefacts) are dropped into an anomalies counter rather than bucketed; bucket sums equal series counts on all 630 HIST lines of this run (verified by script), so no sample was dropped or double-counted.
observer effect (honest labelling): these are INSTRUMENTED-build numbers, not production timings. The stamp-pair floor measured by the overhead phase is a median of 30 cycles per stamp pair across processes (TSC granularity on this host is ~30 cycles: wait p50 lands exactly on 30), and the wait-record update runs inside the following hold. Read single-thread waits as "at the measurement floor" and holds as inflated by at most roughly one stamp pair plus one bucket update (tens of cycles) - immaterial against the hold scales below (60..4490 cycles) and against the contended waits (up to ~30000 cycles at p99). TSC calibration: every process calibrates its own rdtsc against CLOCK_MONOTONIC over 200 ms and prints cyc_per_ns in its META line; all 63 processes measured exactly 2.9944 cyc/ns.
phases: overhead (stamp-pair floor); bench small_churn / mixed_batch / large_batch / mixed_lifecycle (the Z-003/Z-005 baseline workloads, bench_k002.c included UNMODIFIED by the probe, malloc/free entry points only - that is what the baseline workloads call); calloc_churn and realloc_cycle (supplementary entry-point phases, NOT baseline workloads: calloc(1, small sizes) churn, and a realloc grow 64->256->1024->4096 + shrink-to-32 cycle seeded by realloc(NULL,16)); contended small_churn shape at 4 and 8 threads (supplementary, NOT a baseline: Z-020 owns the contention ladder; this phase exists so the wait histogram has a contended specimen in the same run).
load state: /proc/loadavg 2.50 / 3.57 / 2.74 at run start, 2.68 / 3.49 / 2.74 at run end - STEADY MODERATE co-tenant load on this shared 32-thread host, not a quiet window: an Aesir forge mojo test process (1 core at 100%) and a Tensile library build (bursty, --jobs=12) were observed running during the window. The run is therefore labelled SHARED-HOST, NOT QUIET. Its defence is the protocol's own: pinning, median-of-processes, and drift <= 3.0% on every headline series; preemption outliers survive only in the max column (up to ~16.9M cycles on one contended wait sample) and move no median. Baseline-of-record status is claimed for the single-thread hold/wait SHAPE (medians and percentile structure); absolute cycle counts carry the shared-host label.
correctness gate: full make test in this worktree, log hidden_files/z019_make_test.log (team-local, not banked): 4988 checks run (18 smoke + 4970 CHECKS: 29+69+95+23+122+42+4590), 0 failed, 77 by-design SKIP lines, 7/7 RESULT: PASS, make exit 0 - identical counts to the Z-001 baseline. This slice adds no tests; the instrumented build is not part of make test.
zero-cost proof (production build, macro never defined): (1) runner proof block below - production core object 21192 bytes vs instrumented 22336 bytes; nm finds 0 lock_hist symbols in the production object, 2 (snapshot + reset) in the instrumented one. (2) STRONGER: the production object compiled from the patched tree is BYTE-IDENTICAL (cmp clean, 21192 bytes both) to the object compiled from the pre-Z-019 source at git HEAD (09b8e55) with the same flags - the guarded sections preprocess away completely: no code, no data, no branch, no cost.

=== VERDICT - WHERE THE LOCK TIME GOES ===
Single-thread, the lock itself is nearly free and the work under it is the whole story. Uncontended WAIT sits at the measurement floor on every entry point and every baseline workload: mean ~40 cycles (~13 ns), p50 exactly 30 cycles (one TSC tick), p99 60 cycles. Nobody waits for a free lock. HOLD is the path fingerprint, and it scales exactly with what the path does under the lock (median-of-process means, cycles / ns): small-path malloc 60 / 20 and free 86 / 29 (small_churn); mixed_batch malloc 143 / 48 (bimodal: p50 61 for span hits, a span-refill tail pulling p99 to ~2926) and free 601 / 201; large_batch malloc 2542 / 849 and free 4490 / 1499 (the mmap/munmap pair, held across the calls by the S9.4 decision, dominates everything else in the table); mixed_lifecycle malloc 625 / 209 (bimodal small+large mix) and free 1777 / 594. The supplementary entry points agree: calloc hold 65 / 22 (malloc's shape plus zeroing), realloc hold mean 759 / 254 (stay-in-class cases at p50 ~95 cycles; the grow-to-4096 minority pays the large path). Free is the more expensive small entry on every workload - it pays registry/span validation plus, on the mapping path, the cross-thread-owner read and munmap.
Under contention the picture inverts, and this is KL-B sized at histogram level. At 4 threads on one CCD (small_churn shape): hold barely moves (malloc 132, free 203 cycles) but mean WAIT jumps to 1597 cycles (malloc) / 1688 (free) - about 12x the hold - with p99 ~14700-15200 cycles (~5 us). At 8 threads: hold 164 / 279 cycles, mean wait 4190 / 4292 cycles (~1.4 us), about 26x the hold, p50 ~590-654, p99 ~29700-30200 cycles (~10 us). Threads spend their lives queueing for a lock whose actual work takes 100-300 cycles: the scaling wound is serialized HOLD summed across threads, not lock overhead and not hold growth. Any Wave 4/5 redesign is therefore judged by one number first - how much hold it removes from the free and small-malloc paths (TLS cache territory, Z-028/Z-029) and from the large path's mmap-under-lock (KL-C) - because at saturation every cycle of hold is paid once per queued thread as wait.
Gate status: the roadmap gate for Z-019 is "hold/wait histograms for the baseline workloads, recorded" - MET: all four baseline workloads carry malloc and free hold+wait histograms below (7 processes each, checksums clean, drift in bounds), the remaining two public entry points carry supplementary histograms in the same run, and the contended specimen is filed for Z-020 to supersede with its ladder. No performance verdict (WIN/TIE/LOSS) is claimed by this slice: it is a measurement of the banked allocator's lock, and the honest grade of what it shows is the Z-009 baseline's KL-A/KL-B losses explained at lock level.
Bugs: none discovered. One design correction during the build (stale-tag attribution risk in the first draft of the instrumentation) was caught by the team's own risk review before first compile and fixed in the shipped code (tag cleared on unlock); it never existed in a compiled build.
